# Introduction
## by the Author.

---

I foresee that the content of these pages will produce very diverse reactions <note>The original German "Bewegungen" implies emotional stirrings or movements of the mind.</note>. I do not intend to defend myself against any of them; rather, I shall watch the turmoil from my lonely crevice in the rocks with indifference and—remain silent. If only those to whom I raise my voice hear me: then my purpose and success will be achieved exactly where I so ardently desire, and I shall be satisfied. This alone, dear public, is what I wish for, yet also what I fear: I wish for it because I consider this very thing to be the greatest happiness for a mortal; I fear it because it is terrifying to be weighed and found wanting <note>original: "gewogen, und zu leicht erfunden zu werden" — a reference to the biblical "writing on the wall" in Daniel 5:27.</note>.

If I owe you an account of my plan, most esteemed readers, I will provide it to you in the following lines. But if you desire a justification: then allow me to reject this demand! First, acquire the capab-

<summary>In this preface, the author introduces his work with a blend of humility and defiance, expressing a desire to reach a specific audience while remaining indifferent to general public criticism.</summary>
<keywords>author's preface, public reception, literary intent, justification, solitude</keywords>

--- Page 174 ---
<meta>This page continues the word "Fähigkeit" (ability/capability) from the previous page's catchword "Fä-". The catchword "Wun" at the bottom of the transcription suggests the next page begins with a word such as "Wunder" (miracle or wonder).</meta>

ility to judge me, and then summon me before your judgment seat; with the expression and the heart of a philanthropist <note>original: "Menschenfreundes" — literally a "friend of humanity," a term typical of the Enlightenment for someone dedicated to the well-being of others.</note>, I shall answer you. —

The following missive <note>original: "Sendschreiben" — a formal public letter or epistle intended for a wide audience.</note> is intended for men whom you likely did not expect to find on this planet. But they certainly exist. The first pages of this treatise will distinguish them for you. In the following pages, they make themselves known to the physicians and to some genuine students of nature <note>original: "Schülern der Natur" — likely referring to natural philosophers or those who study the physical and metaphysical laws of the world.</note>, and the conclusion partly justifies the reproaches of some modern thinkers, while partly refuting them and calling upon those unknown figures for their defense. Behold, there is my plan!

You will perhaps find the presentation to be visionary <note>original: "schwärmerisch" — a complex term often used in this era to describe excessive or fanatical enthusiasm, but here used by the author to embrace a passionate, inspired style.</note>. So be it! True enthusiasm does not leave a brand of shame. And fire—expanding, illuminating, animating fire—should it not... set things on fire? Could an action remain without effect?

Here you have everything you need for an introduction. Take it as sincerely as I give it! Become truly human, be wise, and take truth as your guide, so that you may calmly and happily penetrate through to the triple light of all knowledge <note>original: "dreyfachen Licht aller Erkenntniß" — an esoteric reference, possibly referring to the light of nature, the light of grace, and the light of glory, or a similar Trinity-based philosophical concept.</note>. Fare you well!

->The Author.<-

Do not wonder, **Sublime Unknowns** <note>original: "Erhabene Unbekannte" — a common term in 18th-century esoteric traditions, such as the Rosicrucians or the Order of the Unknown Philosophers, referring to hidden masters or spiritual guides.</note>, when you see a **profane** <note>original: "Profanen" — in this context, someone who has not yet been initiated into a secret society or sacred mystery.</note> man approaching the hallowed thresholds of your hidden dwellings. Your tender invitations, your repeated signals, your manifold encouragements—which you send forth from time to time to the wandering children of the world with as much honesty as **philanthropy** <note>original: "Menschenliebe" — literally "love of humanity," a central virtue of the Enlightenment and fraternal organizations.</note>—are proofs of your sincerity and your righteous cause that are too strong for any thinking human soul to fail to recognize, or for one to be able to resist you any longer. See, **Sublime Unknowns**, the motive that fixes me here to this **writing desk** in an hour of inspiration, to tell you that among the great crowd of the **profane**, you have a reader who esteems you highly, who honors you, who—understands you. —

Come then, come! hidden **Muse**! Lend me your wings, deep, **sublime wisdom of the unknowns**! Not you, proud but empty creature of trifling wit, to whom almost everyone so blindly pays homage! But—

...s of fortune? <note>The German text starts mid-phrase with "Kinder des Glücks," completing the thought from the previous page regarding these "overjoyed beings."</note> What expression is sublime enough for them? Daily communion with the **Deity**, and unlimited knowledge of His image—the **triple hidden nature** <note>original: "dreyfachen verborgenen Natur" — likely referring to the Hermetic and alchemical triad of Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury, or the mystical triad of Body, Soul, and Spirit.</note>—is their sole occupation; and pure joy, peace of conscience, and abundance of wealth, health, and happy days are the certain fruits thereof. nourish yourselves from the fields of the **spirit-world** with more satisfying objects than the mere investigation of physical nature can provide. What else can creation possess, aside from the enjoyment of that with which **Providence** has gifted you, that would deserve such an expression? Gladly you leave the great ones of the earth to their thrones. ...diligent anatomical experiments certainly prove you to be liars daily, since the **animal spirit** <term>thierischer Geist: In 18th-century physiology, this was believed to be a subtle, volatile fluid that traveled through the nerves to transmit movement and sensation, acting as the bridge between the physical body and the immaterial soul.</term> of which I speak here shows itself clearly and palpably to the sight of anyone who does not wish to be intentionally blind, within the channels of the nerves. They view the **nervous system** as so many extensions, resembling musical strings, from whose more or less tension, more or less elasticity, and more or less *vibration*—if I may borrow this cobweb-expression from them—the various measure of our sensations is also said to depend, according to the respective proportion and connection with the soul. It is just as well that with this **string-philosophy** <term>Saitenphilosophie: A mechanistic view of the human body, common in the 18th century, which compared the nerves to the strings of a musical instrument (like a harpsichord) that vibrate to produce sensations.</term>—which fits so poorly with the colorful **atomic theory** <term>Atomenlehre: Here referring to materialist philosophies that suggested the world and the human body are composed merely of tiny, colliding particles.</term> of their confederates—they do not likewise already dare to explain the essence of sensations themselves, and the manner in which the soul, for instance, perceives when it is awakened from its slumber by the vibration of the nerves! Yet for the existence of the soul, you (or at least most of you) still have some remnants of reverence; although I already know physicians who find delight in [reducing] themselves and their fellow men not merely to the level of cattle—for these, too, still have a soul—but—

[open wide the gates] of **Light**, and not leave the sacred thresholds of the beautiful **Temple of Nature** <note>A common Enlightenment and Romantic metaphor where Nature is viewed as a sacred space requiring spiritual and intellectual initiation to understand.</note> until its sublime guardians find us worthy to enter its interior without hindrance, and to behold mysterious Nature just as she is! Oh, then go away! You were not made to be wise! Nature has denied you the divinity of the earth! Joy is not for you! To be human is, for you, a sentence of death! Ah, go no further, unhappy mortals! Draw back your foot from the edge of the abyss! Tremble [and retreat] beneath the sheltering hills, beneath the dwellings of the **wise Unknown**—abodes unshakable even by the hammer of time and all the destructive tools of nature! Enjoy peace there, feel it—

— — — But! where am I going? In the delirium of my joy, I almost unveiled your secret too much, **Exalted Sages** <note>original: "Erhabene Weise"; a respectful address to the legendary masters or "adepts" of the alchemical tradition.</note>. a life that, here below, was only a

<meta>This page continues the author's polemic against "profane physicians," picking up from the previous page's exhortation to seek spiritual wisdom. The word beginning with "E..." on page 79 likely concludes here as "eternally exempt" (Ewig-überhoben) or a similar title of address.</meta>

...exempt, from rummaging with your anatomical knife from time to time in the entrails of humans and animals, in order to inform yourselves of the causes of a disease which, however, commonly exceeds your limited healing powers. It is indeed good to know the construction of the animal body. It is commendable to be acquainted with the mechanism of animals. Nothing humbles our pride more and makes the infinite wisdom of the Creator more palpable than investigations of this kind.

However, to want to learn the cause of the disease from the dissection <term>dissection<gloss>Zergliederung: the anatomical practice of opening a corpse to study its parts.</gloss></term> of a deceased body is, to put it mildly, folly. Even if you truly believe you have discovered something similar to it: it is always the consequence and not the cause itself. You act like those who, after they had deliberately scared the thief away from their bedroom, entered with a calm expression and conducted an extensive investigation into why they were missing their jewels. <note>This metaphor suggests that the "thief" (the disease or the vital cause) has already left the body at death, leaving only the "missing jewels" (the damage/symptoms) behind for the doctor to find.</note> Do not reproach me for failing to recognize, in my history of disease, the beneficial consequences that the art of dissection obviously has regarding most external accidents <note>original: "äußerlichen Zufälle." In this period, this specifically refers to physical trauma, injuries, or surgical cases as opposed to internal, systemic diseases.</note>! These consequences do not speak on your behalf. They are beneficial and proven only for another part of the healing arts, which you imperiously cast away from yourselves, and over which you, I know not out of...

...on what ridiculous ground you arrogate to yourselves the most absolute office of judge, exactly as if you alone were the owners of Pandora's box <note>In Greek mythology, Pandora’s box contained all the evils of the world. The author suggests physicians act as if they have sole authority over the "evils" of disease.</note>. I mean the art of surgery <term>surgery<gloss>Wundarzneykunst: literally "wound-medicine," the practical craft of treating external injuries, fractures, and physical trauma.</gloss></term>, which truly has far more reliability than your praised art of healing <term>healing arts<gloss>Heilkunde: here referring to theoretical internal medicine, which the author views as less certain than physical surgery.</gloss></term>, and for which anatomical experiments are certainly of the greatest use and therefore perfectly indispensable.

But external accidents, my gentlemen, are also by no means the subject of which I have spoken so far, nor do I intend to speak. These must mostly be remedied through external means as well, and for that reason they do not deserve the name of diseases of the animal spirit <term>animal spirit<gloss>thierischen Geistes: the vital forces or "nerve fluids" thought to mediate between the mind and the physical body.</gloss></term>, to which I have solely restricted myself until now.

This, my dear physicians, is the lesson I wanted to give you before your departure, and now you may go! I am done with you for this time, and whether I will have more leisure or inclination in the future to concern myself with your instruction, I doubt very much. Take this, then, as willingly as I give it to you, and do not believe that I have wasted even a single syllable in vain or without deliberation. It is perhaps not yet day in your soul; but it will come, it will most certainly come, and with its gracious light, it will clear up and illuminate all the great darkness of your understanding.

<summary>The author concludes his lecture to the physicians, distinguishing between the valid practical utility of surgery for physical injuries and the physicians' failure to understand internal "diseases of the spirit." According to the respective sensations, passions, and desires that take the upper hand in your soul from time to time, the animal spirit—and through it its dwelling, the machine of your body—always enjoys either a state of illness or the charms of good, lasting health.

[over]come, wound, kill, and make alive again. <note>The text completes the thought from the previous page regarding the cycle of life and death.</note>
This very sad cycle, this very struggle, this effort of the invisible, living **primordial force** to triumph over its enemies, is now precisely that—so they say further—which we call the **present laws of nature** of our planet, and whose imitation we so strongly recommend to our students. These are the very same laws which you find praised with almost exaggerated **enthusiasm** in our numerous writings. —
But what do these laws consist of, how do they work, and what is the purpose and success in such a strange contrast of causes and effects? — On this, too, you will find enlightenment and instruction in the **School of the Wise Unknown**. <note>The "Wise Unknown" likely refers to the "Secret Chiefs" or hidden masters often cited in 18th-century Rosicrucian and alchemical traditions.</note> From these exalted men we know—and in their secret assemblies it is even made clear and distinct to the point of visual evidence—that not only in all **sublunary** <term>sublunary: literally "under the moon," a term used in the old geocentric model of the universe to describe the earthly realm of change, decay, and the four elements.</term> things, but also especially in the atmosphere of our globe itself, there exists a certain, extremely fine and delicate **light-essence**, as a ray or spark of the **Deity**, if I may express myself so, which may be called the true **source of life** of Nature. For this heavenly, invisible light is the **soul of the world** <note>original: "Seele der Welt", or *Anima Mundi*—the concept that a spiritual essence permeates all physical matter.</note>, the **mainspring** of all movements, the cause of all births, and the life of all beings that inhabit it. Its presence animates everything, and its absence [withers/destroys]... <meta>The text cuts off at the prefix "ver-", likely intended to be "vernichtet" (destroys) or "vergeht" (passes away).</meta>

<summary>This page defines the laws of nature as a struggle of a divine primordial force and introduces the "soul of the world"—an invisible, celestial light-essence that animates all earthly life.</summary>

**Wind in its belly**, as the great **Hermes** expresses it; <note>original: "Wind in seinem Bauche" – A reference to the *Emerald Tablet* (*Tabula Smaragdina*), a foundational alchemical text attributed to Hermes Trismegistus, which states: "The wind carried it in its belly."</note> there it mingles with the finest vapors of the water and other **effluvia** <note>original: "Effluvien" – invisible emissions or vapors believed to flow out of all physical bodies.</note> rising from the earth, which are constantly present in the **atmosphere** in the greatest abundance, and finally comes down to earth in the form of a still invisible, **spiritual salt** in the water. <note>original: "geistlichen Salzes" – In this context, "spiritual" refers to something subtle, volatile, or non-physical, rather than religious. This "salt" was seen as the first physical manifestation of the celestial spirit.</note> In this state, it is **All in All**, a **primal substance** of all composite beings in nature. 

In the earth, however, where it encounters the already prepared **seeds** of bodies and a great multitude of other specifying beings, it is robbed of its **universality** and is transformed by the active **soul** of these bodies (which is thereby set free) into all those things that we perceive daily in every **kingdom of nature**. By dissolving the **seeds** of the bodies back into their first origin, it is seized by their **fire**—which has been liberated and is already more embodied—taken into its union, and likewise embodied through a **cooking** <note>original: "Kochung" – Alchemists used "cooking" or "decoction" to describe the natural process of maturation and refinement of matter via heat, whether in the earth or the laboratory.</note> specific to each body. It thus dissolves the seed; the seed, however, which initially acted passively, now becomes active, and after it has sufficiently impregnated itself with the dissolving agent, it **coagulates** the same and thus yields a thousandfold fruit in its autumn. Herein lies and consists now

**is** the entire work, and the true purpose of **Nature**. For she receives from the general womb of the heavens the animating **fire**, the **aerial saltpeter** <note>original: "Luftsalpeter" – Aerial nitre. Early natural philosophers believed a vital, salt-like substance in the air was responsible for life and combustion.</note>, the **universal acid** <note>original: "Saure" – In this context, "acid" refers to a primary reactive principle or "sharp" spirit in the atmosphere rather than a liquid chemical.</note>, the hidden **food of life**, the great **universal agent** <note>original: "Weltagens" – A hypothetical force or substance that drives all physical and chemical changes in the universe.</note>, or whatever else one wishes to call it; she gathers it together from a seemingly infinite dispersion and, through a slow **cooking** <note>original: "Kochung" – Also known as decoction; the alchemical term for the process by which nature or an artist applies gentle heat to ripen or mature a substance.</note>, combines it with a certain species of earth, which is thereby refined, intimately dissolved, crushed, as it were **ensouled** <note>original: "begeistert" – Literally "spirited." This refers to the process of infusing "dead" matter with vital spirit or life-force.</note>, and finally raised to the extreme degree of fineness.

This refinement of the earth through fire, in which the entire secret of **Nature** consists, has its preordained limits, however, which are very different with respect to the **three kingdoms** <note>original: "drey Reiche" – The Animal, Vegetable, and Mineral kingdoms.</note> and their numerous products; and that very body in which the aforementioned refinement of the earth has already reached the highest degree has always been called the **seed** <note>original: "Same" – In alchemical theory, the "seed" is the most concentrated, potent form of a substance, representing its peak of natural development.</note> of that same thing. For the refinement can never surpass the seed of an individual; here it has reached its prescribed goal, at which **Nature** suddenly stands still and rests completely. The **seed** is therefore the most perfect body in the sequence of things, which **Nature** now offers to **Art** <note>original: "Kunst" – This refers to Alchemy, often called "The Art." The text suggests that nature brings a substance to its natural peak (the seed), and then the Alchemist takes over to further exalt it.</note>, and which she is no longer capable of bringing any higher.

**is.** <note>Completing the thought from the previous page: "...and that very body in which the aforementioned refinement of the earth has already reached [its peak] is..."</note> \*) The internal diversity of **seed** is, however, as it were infinite, since every individual capable of **reproduction** has its own particular seed. In the seed of plants, on the other hand, the vegetable germ is already more and more tightly enclosed, so that in some species of this seed, the animating **earth-salt** <note>original: "Erdsalz" – A vital mineral principle believed to reside in the soil and trigger the growth of life.</note> often has work to do for months to open its shell and properly unlock it.

\*) The **plant kingdom** can convince us of this truth most emphatically. # 104

Sages. They silently assume that the mechanical manipulations of **common chemistry** <note>German: *Scheidekunst*, literally "the art of separation," an early term for chemistry.</note> are already known to their apprentice; for this reason, they pass over them for the most part in silence. But not the **aqua regia** <term>aqua regia: original "Königswasser" (literally "royal water"), a highly corrosive mixture of nitric and hydrochloric acids used by early chemists to dissolve "noble" metals like gold.</term> or the **spirit of nitre** <term>spirit of nitre: original "Salpetergeist," known today as nitric acid.</term> used by common **bunglers** <note>original: "Pfuscher," a derogatory term used by alchemists for laboratory workers who used crude, violent chemical methods instead of "natural" ones.</note>, with which they merely tear bodies into small pieces instead of opening them naturally while preserving their internal form,

now, whether considered in its natural state or as something artificially prepared for our work, it is nonetheless very different from the common variety. For:

1) The common kind is found only here and there in the mines of the earth. Ours, however, is to be met with everywhere, as no thing in the whole world is found without it.

2) The common kind is a product of nature; ours is a **work of art**, produced from two **saline beings** <term>saline beings: original "salinischen Wesen," referring to substances of a salt-like or mineral nature that form the base of the alchemical solvent.</term> which have one and the same origin and root.

3) The common kind does not take upon itself the nature and properties of all metals when it is joined with them; but ours does.

4) The common kind is a fluid, moving, running being on every surface. Ours is a dry thing—that is, a dry, thickened water of a salty nature, wherein the property of **Earth** has gained the upper hand over the **Water** in its mixture.

5) The common kind is cold and moist; ours is hot and fiery.

6) The more the common kind is **distilled** <term>distilled: original "destillirt," the process of heating a substance to separate its volatile components, then condensing the vapor.</term>, the more

...wise masters have found, through the most infallible experience, to be perfectly true and established: that this proposition only actually holds true for those types of seeds whose inner germ is preformed with the future <term>organism</term> <note>original: *Organismus*. In this 18th-century context, it refers to the complex, structured arrangement of a living being.</note> of the animal or plant for which each seed is specified, in a manner that is indeed invisible, yet entirely sufficient for the eye of the true <term>naturalist</term> <note>original: *Naturforscher*. Gold itself is thus nothing more than an earthly, colored, heavy mass, composed of nothing but such parts that have no organism at all; a mere <term>aggregate</term> <note>original: *Aggregat*. In early chemistry and alchemy, this refers to a collection of particles held together by physical force rather than a living, biological structure.</note> that holds together so firmly only because of its natural purity and because of the great uniformity and <term>fixity</term> <note>original: *Fixität*. The quality of being "fixed"—meaning the substance can withstand the highest heat of the fire without evaporating or changing.</note> of its components. If this body were of the same nature as the seeds of the animal and plant kingdoms, we could certainly never promise ourselves more than perhaps a mere <term>physical multiplication</term> <note>original: *körperliche Vermehrung*. Alchemical operations that aim to change a specific amount of base metal into gold, as opposed to the "Universal" work of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, but more than...

--- Page 300 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 133 regarding the nature of gold as an aggregate rather than an organism. The author is explaining that while gold is physically "fixed," its internal essence can be liberated and refined through alchemical processes to a state far superior to common gold.</meta>

Gold would, in this case, be absolutely impossible. However, the Sages maintain—and for them, as previously stated, it is mathematically proven through the hallmark of the most infallible experience—that whenever they dissolve gold in its proper <term>menstruum</term> <note>original: *Menstruum*. In alchemy, this refers to a solvent—the "liquid of the month"—capable of breaking a solid body down into its primary essence.</note> and then concentrate it again, they never recover merely physical gold. Instead, they obtain a <term>tinctural essence</term> <note>original: *tinkturalisches Wesen*. A refined, spiritualized form of matter that has the power to "tinge" or transform other substances into its own high quality.</note> that is several thousand times more perfect. Thus, if one wishes to obtain common gold, this essence must first be "degraded" back to the state of gold by mixing it with base metals. From this, it becomes clear that there is an even higher degree of refinement for the <term>primordial metallic earth</term> <note>original: *metalli-schen Grunderde*. The fundamental, foundational matter that serves as the "soil" or base for all metallic growth.</note> than that which Nature has achieved in the composition of common gold. Regarding this further refinement, the wise masters tell us that it can be pushed so far through repeated dissolutions that the gold eventually ceases to be gold at all. It finally attains the same fineness and penetrability as <term>fire-essence</term> <note>original: *Feuerwesen*. A term for matter in its most subtle, energetic state, so refined it behaves like light or heat.</note> itself; consequently, it can no longer be contained by any vessel, no matter how dense it may be. This, they say, is precisely why one must only push the "multiplication of quality" up to a certain degree. Here you have the true explanation of the aforementioned riddle. From this, you see that the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: *Philosophen*. A common shorthand for "Alchemists" or "Philosophers of Nature."</note>, in the performance of their miraculous work, [follow] Na-

[Na]ture can indeed by no means do without <note>This sentence fragment completes a thought from the previous page, suggesting that while Nature provides the raw material, she requires the intervention of the alchemist.</note>, but that they compel her to proceed further in her business than she would have gone if left to her own devices. In alchemical texts, "Art" refers to the skilled practice of alchemy itself, which mimics and accelerates natural processes.</note>: then descend into the subterranean caverns of the mountains, and you will observe without great effort that, because it is impossible for Nature to manufacture the *Mercury of the Sages* within the earth—and even if this were possible for her, she could still by no means bring *gold* into proper contact with it; or if one were to grant both, she would still lack the necessary vessel, the proper sealing, and the required *incubation fire*—that, I say, it must remain forever impossible for her, in her present state, to arrive on her own at that point which she can reach under other circumstances when supported by Art. A term popularized by Paracelsus referring to the outward physical marks or characteristics that reveal the hidden, inner essence or divine purpose of a substance.</note>

<meta>This page continues the author's comparison between nature's slow processes and the alchemist's "Art." It follows from the previous page's discussion on the refinement of metallic "earths."</meta>

mines produce gold; Art, on the other hand, through a far more convenient arrangement of both the means and the necessary tools, produces even more than mere physical gold—namely, the multiplying <term>Gold-stone of the Sages</term> <note>original: *Goldstein der Weisen*. This is a specific term for the Philosopher's Stone, emphasizing its ability to "multiply" or increase the quantity of gold it can produce.</note>. The means to achieve this intended refinement consists of a special kind of <term>fermentation</term> <note>original: *Gährung*. In alchemy, fermentation is the process that gives the "Stone" the power to transform or "leaven" base metals into gold.</note>, or, in the language of the Sages, a continuous <term>cooking</term> <note>original: *Kochung*. Also known as decoction; the application of steady, controlled heat over a long period to mature the substance.</note>. This cooking achieves everything at once which certain <term>envious ones</term> <note>original: *Mißgünstige*. A common term in alchemical literature (Latin: *invidiosi*) referring to authors who intentionally obscured the truth by describing the single process as many complex, separate steps to confuse the uninitiated.</note> have described in their books as a hundred different tasks, as if they were so many treatment methods essential to the essence of the matter. 

For this single, continuous cooking is what the philosophers mean when they make such a fuss regarding the <term>subsequent work</term> <note>original: *Nacharbeit*.</note> about <term>dissolving</term> <note>original: *Solviren* / *solutio*.</note>, <term>digesting</term> <note>original: *Digeriren* / *digestio*.</note>, <term>putrefying</term> <note>original: *Fäulen* / *putrefactio*.</note>, <term>distilling</term> <note>original: *Destilliren* / *destillatio*.</note>, <term>purifying</term> <note>original: *Reinigen* / *purgatio*.</note>, and <term>incerating</term> <note>original: *Inceriren* / *inceratio*. The process of softening a dry substance to a waxy consistency.</note>; or when they speak of making the matter **black, white, and red** <note>These colors—*nigredo*, *albedo*, and *rubedo*—represent the primary stages of the alchemical Great Work.</note>, and other such works which they call their own. 

It is this quite simple cooking that transforms the matter, reveals the constituents of the bodies without displacing them from their center, unites them anew, and finally raises them into the **heaven of the philosophers**. Without it, no rebirth of the seeds from **water and spirit** <note>This uses biblical language to describe the alchemical union of the volatile "spirit" (often mercury) and the "water" or solvent.</note> could occur, nor any ennobling of the matter, and certainly no improvement of the lower metals could take place.

...for which you thirst with such desire and such great longing. Moreover, I do not need to first prove that all these effects are only possible in the moist <note>original: *im Feuchten*. <meta>This page continues the instructional sequence from page 144. While the previous page focused on the internal preparation of the seeker—arming oneself with patience and studying the "Stone" theoretically in one's head—this page introduces the "Third Rule" regarding the rigorous comparison of sources and the total commitment required for the Great Work.</meta>

This is the third rule which the wise masters prescribe to you. First, bring all genuine authors into the most precise <term>concordance</term> <note>original: *Concordanz*. In alchemical study, this refers to the practice of comparing different, often cryptic, texts to find the underlying points where all authors agree, believing this "common ground" to be the actual truth.</note>, compare them without prejudice, and from the matching result of all their instructions, warnings, and <term>precautions</term> <note>original: *Kautelen*. This term suggests formal or technical safeguards used to avoid errors or deception in the process.</note>, form your plan. In doing so, do not allow yourself the slightest gullibility, but test this plan more than once in the strictest manner; view it from all sides, raise objections against it, and answer them again; and if you are still satisfied with it and find it to be in agreement with the instructions of all <term>Adepts</term>: only then will it be time for you to lay your hand to the work, and seek to accomplish through experience that which you have found to be true and correct in theory. 

However, should you stand in such a relationship with civil society—to which either birth or fate has chained you—that it leaves you little leisure or opportunity to venture with seriousness into the business of the wise: then all the aforementioned advantages would still be of no use to you. For these sublime men have recorded it as a <term>maxim of experience</term> <note>original: *Erfahrungssatz*. A rule or principle derived from practical observation rather than pure theory.</note> that their <term>masterpiece</term> <note>original: *Kunststück*. A reference to the "Great Work" or the completion of the Philosopher's Stone, which was considered the ultimate "piece of art."</note> requires the whole person, and is by no means compatible with other side-pursuits or things that fall within the business of common life. Therefore, also imagine

Thus, this reproach does not apply to the Order at all, because it has indeed published many writings from which even the profane <note>original: *Profanen*; in Masonic terminology, this refers to those outside the secret society</note> could gather treasures of truth and knowledge if they wished. And as for the practical application—well, they can experience that within the Order itself, provided they have the necessary disposition and ability.

Moreover, I believe I am entitled to the suspicion that the author, upon reading this anecdote, will pity my measure of gullibility and simplicity. But I ask him, with the same moderation that he has almost entirely maintained in his judicial opinion on the origin and purpose of the Order, to consult as a theologian the fourth book of Arndt's *True Christianity* <note>original: *Arnds wahren Christenthum*; *Vier Bücher vom wahren Christenthum* (1605) by Johann Arndt, a foundational text for Lutheran Pietism and Christian mysticism</note> regarding a subject that currently lies beyond his sphere, and which...

With the exclusion of all other undertakings relating to this, I will now seek to make myself worthy of your blessing and your goodwill first, before I—as I have done until now upon a wretched whim—set out into the open sea to sail for an island which, like the fairy castles in the air, only becomes visible when you either gift the mariner with a magical guide, or provide him with a special knowledge of the philosophical waters <note>a metaphor for the complex and transformative stages of the alchemical process</note> and all prevailing winds, and dangerous spots, sandbanks, and cliffs threatening destruction; and indeed, in no step-motherly <note>meaning "not in a meager or stingy way"</note>

<meta>This page continues from page 194, completing the sentence regarding the "measure" of the Order's gifts. The author continues their petition to the secret society, praising their curriculum and acknowledging the strict requirements for entry.</meta>

measure, enriched. — You have made the first steps toward your hidden **Sanctuary of Nature** a nursery for almost all branches of science, and at the same time decreed that no one who has not first completed the **English grades** <note>original: "englischen Grade"; likely referring to foundational degrees of initiation, possibly influenced by the English Freemasonic systems which served as a prerequisite for higher "Internal" or "Scottish" degrees in 18th-century Germany</note> and their accompanying trials should ever dare to hope to ascend higher, or to penetrate into the **Holy of Holies** <term>Allerheiligsten: the innermost and most sacred sanctum of a temple, referring here to the ultimate secrets of alchemy and nature</term> of your magnificent **Temple of Nature**.

Well then, great and more than venerable Sages! Since the welfare of your erring fellow citizens lies so close to your heart, and since you desire with such warmth that everyone might find the way to your mysterious light: then prove the sincerity of this desire by initiating me, at the very least as the lowliest of your **apprentices**, into this **secret school**! You have established the **fear of God**, **virtue**, **thirst for knowledge**, and **submissiveness** <term>Beugsamkeit: a willingness to be guided and to submit one's ego to the instructions of the masters</term> as the fourfold condition under which you grant a **profane** <term>Profanen: a person outside the order; the uninitiated</term> entry into your praiseworthy **Order**—a condition that reveals the holiness and the great internal worth of your intentions to every sincere researcher at first glance.

For a long time now, I have been convinced of the truth and correctness of this, your premise. With amazement, I have on several occasions witnessed the successful outcome of your great [efforts], which are free from any shadow of **self-interest**

<summary>The author acknowledges the rigorous moral and intellectual prerequisites—fear of God, virtue, curiosity, and submissiveness—required to enter the secret Order. He petitions the "venerable Sages" for initiation as a lowly apprentice into their "Temple of Nature."</summary>

...of the truth and correctness of these far-off efforts, watching from my lonely residence, and I have strived to make myself capable of practicing these pleasant and **peace-bringing duties** as well as was possible for me without a guide. Thus, from this side as well, you have no obstacle to overcome, should you most kindly decide to open your **holy gates** to me.

—Furthermore, it is not unknown to me that, due to certain circumstances and chance events, you found yourselves on various occasions under the necessity of weaving other **systems** with your **holy institute** <term>Institute: referring to the core organization or foundational structure of the secret order</term>. This was done partly to better conceal your true intentions and to learn the capabilities of your pupils more reliably, and partly to usefully occupy with **secondary matters** <term>Nebendingen: peripheral rituals or studies that do not lead to the core secrets</term> those people who possessed neither the necessary **organization** <note>original: "Organisation"; in the 18th century, this referred to a person’s physical, mental, or spiritual constitution and their fitness for specific work</note> to grasp your **secret dogmas**, nor the inclination and strength to practice the prescribed **duties**, yet to whom—for well-known reasons—entry into the **Order** could not be refused. 

It is also not unknown to me that not only the aforementioned circumstances, but also certain other **incidental events** <term>Nebeneräugnisse: side-occurrences or historical accidents that shaped the order's development</term> have given existence to many **grades** or **steps** of your venerable **Order**. According to the personal **confession** of **Masonic writers**, some of these might actually be dispensable, as they not infrequently arose entirely without your prior knowledge or approval...

...to seem to be. *) Since you are not concerned with the quantity of members, but only with a select number, and you have already sufficiently recognized from what I have said so far in the contents of this **missive** <term>Sendschreiben: a formal letter or circular, often used for instructional or philosophical communication</term> the class to which the modest capabilities of my spirit might belong: so it is left entirely to your own wise discretion which **degree** <note>original: "Stuffe"; literally a step or rung, referring to the levels of initiation</note> of your illustrious **Order** you wish to recognize as appropriate for my spirit. <meta>This page continues the author’s philosophical justification for why secret societies maintain "surface" levels or public-facing rituals to protect their inner truths from the general public. It follows the previous page's discussion of "public lodges" being suitable for the "great mass."</meta>

...mass of the brainless <note>original: "Haufen ohne Gehirn"; a disparaging reference to those who do not seek deep philosophical truth</note>, they may even be necessary for its condition, these offspring of a **thirst for innovation** <term>Neuerungssucht: an obsessive desire for new things or reforms, often used critically in the 18th century to describe superficial changes</term>, of self-interest, and occasionally also of bizarre chance and fatal necessity—to be forced, despite the most just and benevolent cause, to hide beneath dark clouds from the general **rapacity** <term>Raubsucht: a predatory greed or desire to seize and expose things</term> of men. All this may, on the whole, have its advantages, its good and perhaps irreproachable sides. However, if it is no crime to do justice to the feeling of one's own consciousness, and if it is true—as I feel firmly convinced—that you let your pupils climb around on the surface of things for a while only so that you may prepare them for the interior of the same with all the more leisure and security, or [to let] the same [bring itself] forth
->to<-

---

,, [ab]solute <note>The OCR "schränkter" is likely "unumschränkter," a common title in high-degree Masonry</note> **Commander of the Temple**, **Sublime Philosopher**, **Phoenix**, **Knight Kadosh**, **Scot of Saint Andrew of Scotland**, and **Knight of Palestine**." The latter two are the only ones the author calls true degrees, as those which indeed contained the secret, the purpose, and the essence of **Masonry** <note>original: "Maurerey"</note>. "If I," he says on page 129, "were not even in a position to judge for myself the merit of the truths which these two classes contain: I would still have the highest opinion of them because of the great caution with which they are bestowed, because of the small number of persons to whom they are granted, and because of the strict selection to which one is bound. One must necessarily value things that one sees treated with dignity and without desecration. Everything that is abandoned to the **rapacity** of curiosity, and of which descriptions are in the hands of the whole world, indicates only vain objects, etc."
->Editor’s Note<-

<meta>This page continues the author’s passionate plea to his mentors or masters. to seek <note>The text continues from the previous page's sentiment about preparing a pupil to seek the interior truth.</note>, and makes them capable and eager, if, I say, these assumptions do not deceive me: then you cannot possibly view this last expression as a consequence of **pride** or **self-love**, but will instead feel compelled by my **frankness** <term>Offenherzigkeit: an 18th-century virtue of being open, sincere, and transparent in one's intentions</term> to frankly forgive me for the daring language with which I ventured to write down the aforementioned reminder, and to reach out your fatherly hands to me in a matter where, without you, without your kind instruction, all my laboriously gained knowledge would be entirely as if it were not. Yes, I see it in advance, that you will certainly reach this conclusion, so desired by me and so appropriate to your benevolent character. Ah! with anxious delight and a heart leaping for joy, I weep toward this happy day. Let it soon, soon let it rise for me, this sun awaited with full longing! Bring it up soon over my still extremely darkened soul, the day of **Enlightenment** <term>Aufklärung: the intellectual movement of the 18th century that sought to replace superstition with reason and "light"</term> and of the wisdom-granting light! Look—with the utmost force I must tear myself away from you. Oh, if you could perceive it as vividly, feel it as strongly and as deeply, as this heart beating in my bosom feels it—how hard and sad it is to be gifted with a burning **thirst for knowledge** <term>Wißbegierde: a strong, active, and noble desire to learn and understand the truth</term> and an irresistible inclination toward research,

<meta>This page continues the author’s passionate address to his mentors. Picking up from the previous page's description of an intense "thirst for knowledge," the author uses a vivid medical metaphor of a fever-stricken patient to illustrate his desperate need for spiritual instruction before deciding to end his letter out of respect for his superiors.</meta>

...nevertheless to find rest and satisfaction in no corner of **Creation** <term>Schöpfung: the physical and spiritual universe as a divine work</term>; if you felt it strongly enough, I say, how sad the state of the feverish person is when, in the most violent stage of a **paroxysm** <term>Paroxismus: a medical term for a sudden attack or the peak of a disease; here used metaphorically for the author's intense spiritual agitation</term>, racked by a consuming, glowing fire, he looks in vain for the refreshment of a soothing liquid: then you would certainly not hesitate to approach his parched tongue with a bowl full of your priceless **water of life** <term>Lebenswasser: an alchemical and esoteric term for the "aqua vitae" or spiritual truth that grants eternal life to the soul</term>. 

Yet, I offend you, sublime, venerable men, by speaking to you in this tone. If it is already a crime to merely suspect an honest man of having the capacity to be dishonest, then regarding you, it is utter madness, or the highest degree of injustice and insult—and I would not want to be guilty of either for anything in the world. 

To keep myself safe from this dangerous cliff, I will take revenge on myself for your sake, you invaluable **friends of humanity** <term>Menschenfreunde: an 18th-century term for philanthropists or those dedicated to the betterment of mankind</term>, by immediately—with a hand that resists, yet with firm determination—laying down my **quill** <note>original: "Kiel," meaning a quill pen made from a bird's feather</note>. 

But — yet — 

Yes, so be it!

<summary>The author describes his agonizing spiritual "fever" and begs his mentors for the "water of life" (esoteric knowledge) before abruptly deciding to end his writing to avoid appearing disrespectful to his superiors.</summary>
